          <p>(1) if the ticket reseller did not use a fully completed exemption certificate to claim the exemption from tax for resale, but instead paid tax on the original purchase, then the ticket reseller may do one of the following:</p>
          <p>(i) seek a refund of that tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.50">289A.50</a>; or</p>
          <p>(ii) pass through to the purchaser the amount of the tax the ticket reseller paid on the original purchase, by giving the purchaser credit for the Minnesota state and local tax paid by the ticket reseller on the ticket reseller's original purchase of the ticket. Credit for the tax cannot exceed either the sales tax paid on the original price of the ticket or the sales tax charged by the ticket reseller to the final purchaser;</p>
          <p>(2) if the ticket reseller did not pay tax on the original purchase, tax is due on the full amount of the ticket when resold, without a credit given to the final purchaser; and</p>
          <p>(3) the ticket reseller must retain records documenting the price and tax paid by the ticket reseller when purchasing the ticket and the price and tax collected when the ticket reseller resells the ticket.</p>
          <p>(b) When a ticket reseller who purchased a ticket from a seller who is not in the business of selling tickets resells the ticket, the ticket reseller must charge tax on the total amount for which the ticket is resold and the following rules apply:</p>
          <p>(1) the ticket reseller may credit its purchaser an amount equal to the tax the ticket reseller would have paid its seller, had the seller been registered to collect tax on its sale of the ticket to the ticket reseller. Credit for the tax cannot exceed either the sales tax paid on the original price of the ticket or the sales tax charged by the ticket reseller to the final purchaser. It is presumed that the original purchase price of the ticket is the face amount of the ticket;</p>
          <p>(2) if no tax was paid on the original purchase, tax is due on the full amount of the ticket when resold, without a credit given to the ticket reseller's purchaser; and</p>
          <p>(3) the ticket reseller must retain records documenting the price and tax paid by the ticket reseller when purchasing the ticket and the price and tax collected when the ticket reseller resells the ticket.</p>
          <p>(c) For purposes of this subdivision, "ticket reseller" means a person who:</p>
          <p>(1) purchases admission tickets to a sporting event, theater, musical performance, or place of public entertainment or amusement of any kind;</p>
          <p>(2) resells admission tickets to events under clause (1); and</p>
          <p>(3) is registered to collect tax under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.68.44">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 44.<span class="headnote">Greater Minnesota business expansions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Purchases and use of tangible personal property or taxable services by a qualified business are exempt if:</p>
          <p>(1) the commissioner of employment and economic development certifies to the commissioner of revenue, in a format approved by the commissioner of revenue, that the qualified business meets the requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8738">116J.8738</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) the business subsidy agreement provides that the exemption under this subdivision applies;</p>
          <p>(3) the property or services are primarily used or consumed at the facility in greater Minnesota identified in the business subsidy agreement; and</p>
          <p>(4) the purchase was made and delivery received during the duration of the business subsidy agreement.</p>
          <p>(b) Purchase and use of construction materials and supplies used or consumed in, and equipment incorporated into, the construction of improvements to real property in greater Minnesota are exempt if the improvements after completion of construction are to be used in the conduct of the trade or business of the qualified business, and the commissioner of employment and economic development certifies to the commissioner of revenue, in a format approved by the commissioner of revenue, that the qualified business meets the requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116J.8738">116J.8738</a>. This exemption applies regardless of whether the purchases are made by the business or a contractor.</p>
          <p>(c) The exemptions under this subdivision apply to a local sales and use tax.</p>
          <p>(d) The tax on purchases imposed under this subdivision must be imposed and collected as if the rate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62">297A.62</a> applied, and then refunded in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.75">297A.75</a>. The total amount refunded for a facility over the certification period is limited to the amount listed in the business subsidy agreement. No more than $7,000,000 may be refunded in a fiscal year for all purchases under this subdivision. Refunds must be allocated on a first-come, first-served basis. If more than $7,000,000 of eligible claims are made in a fiscal year, claims by qualified businesses carry over to the next fiscal year, and the commissioner of revenue must first allocate refunds to qualified businesses eligible for a refund in the preceding fiscal year. Any portion of the balance of funds allocated for refunds under this paragraph does not cancel and shall be carried forward to and available for refunds in subsequent fiscal years. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.75">297A.75</a>, subdivision 4, for an eligible refund claim that carries over to a subsequent fiscal year, the interest on the amount carried over must be paid on the refund no sooner than from 90 days after July 1 of the fiscal year in which funds are available for the eligible claim.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.68.45">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 45.<span class="headnote">Jukebox music.</span></h2>
          <p>The purchase of music, either as a digital audio work or in tangible form such as a record or compact disc, by operators that provide the service of making available jukeboxes as amusement devices, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.61#stat.297A.61.3">297A.61, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (g), clause (1), is exempt if the music is used exclusively for the jukebox.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.68.46">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 46.<span class="headnote">Amenities included with the privilege of admission.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The sale of amenities, including but not limited to food and beverages, parking services, and promotional items, that are included in the sales price of the privilege of admission to athletic events and places of amusement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.61#stat.297A.61.3">297A.61, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (m), are exempt when sold by a seller of the privilege of admission that is a professional sports team competing in Major League Baseball, Major League Soccer, the National Basketball Association, the Women's National Basketball Association, the National Football League, or the National Hockey League.</p>
          <p>(b) Under this subdivision, the exempt portion of the sale of the privilege of admission is equal to the purchase price of the amenity if sales or use tax was paid on the amenity when purchased by the seller.</p>
          <p>(c) The seller must retain records documenting the price and tax paid by the seller when purchasing the amenities and the price and tax collected when the seller sells the privilege of admission.</p>
          <p>(d) This subdivision expires July 1, 2030.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.68.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.12.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 12</a>,<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.44.3">44 subd 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/490/#laws.8.8.0">2000 c 490 art 8 s 8</a>,14,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.6.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 6</a>,45-54,<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1Sp/5/#laws.12.93.0">93</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/377/#laws.3.11.0">2002 c 377 art 3 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/377/#laws.9.12.0">art 9 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.1.23.0">2003 c 127 art 1 s 23</a>-25;<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.6.10.0"> art 6 s 10</a>,<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.6.11.0">11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/21/#laws.1.13.0">1Sp2003 c 21 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1Sp/21/#laws.2.8.0">art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.7.14.0">2005 c 151 art 7 s 14</a>-17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/3/#laws.5.8.0">1Sp2005 c 3 art 5 s 8</a>-11; art 7 s 8,9; art 10 s 12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/259/#laws.6.16.0">2006 c 259 art 6 s 16</a>,17; art 13 s 6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.12.29.0">2008 c 154 art 12 s 29</a>-31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/389/#laws.4.4.0">2010 c 389 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/7/#laws.3.6.0">1Sp2011 c 7 art 3 s 6</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/287/#laws.3.50.0">2012 c 287 art 3 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.8.25.0">2013 c 143 art 8 s 25</a>-28; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/150/#laws.2.2.0">2014 c 150 art 2 s 2</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.3.8.0">2014 c 308 art 3 s 8</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.64.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.3.20.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 3 s 20</a>-24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.97.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 97</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.1.67.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 1 s 67</a>; art 14 s 1-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.5.6.0">2023 c 64 art 5 s 6</a>,7; art 6 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.69">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.69 AGRICULTURAL EXEMPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.69.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The gross receipts from the sale of, and storage, distribution, use, or consumption of the items contained in this section are specifically exempted from the taxes imposed by this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.69.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Materials consumed in agricultural production.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Materials stored, used, or consumed in agricultural production of personal property intended to be sold ultimately at retail are exempt, whether or not the item becomes an ingredient or constituent part of the property produced. Materials that qualify for this exemption include, but are not limited to, the following:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) feeds, seeds, trees, fertilizers, and herbicides, including when purchased for use by farmers in a federal or state farm or conservation program;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) materials sold to a veterinarian to be used or consumed in the care, medication, and treatment of agricultural production animals and horses;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) chemicals, including chemicals used for cleaning food processing machinery and equipment;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) materials, including chemicals, fuels, and electricity purchased by persons engaged in agricultural production to treat waste generated as a result of the production process;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) fuels, electricity, gas, and steam used or consumed in the production process, including electricity, gas, or steam used for space heating, cooling, or lighting of facilities housing agricultural animals;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) petroleum products and lubricants;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) packaging materials, including returnable containers used in packaging food and beverage products; and</p>
          <p class="in">(8) accessory tools and equipment that are separate detachable units with an ordinary useful life of less than 12 months used in producing a direct effect upon the product.</p>
          <p class="bl">Machinery, equipment, implements, tools, accessories, appliances, contrivances, and furniture and fixtures, except those listed in this clause are not included within this exemption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.69.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Repair and replacement parts.</span></h2>
          <p>Repair and replacement parts, except tires, used for maintenance or repair of farm machinery, logging equipment, and aquaculture production equipment are exempt, if the part replaces a machinery part assigned a specific or generic part number by the manufacturer of the machinery.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.69.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Machinery, equipment, and fencing.</span></h2>
          <p>The following machinery, equipment, and fencing is exempt:</p>
          <p>(1) farm machinery;</p>
          <p>(2) logging equipment, including chain saws used for commercial logging;</p>
          <p>(3) fencing used for the containment of farmed Cervidae, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/35.153#stat.35.153.3">35.153, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) primary and backup generator units used to generate electricity for the purpose of operating farm machinery, aquacultural production equipment, or logging equipment, or providing light or space heating necessary for the production of livestock, dairy animals, dairy products, or poultry and poultry products; and</p>
          <p>(5) aquaculture production equipment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.69.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p> MS 2004 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.1.34.0">2003 c 127 art 1 s 34</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.69.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Horses; related materials.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Horses, including racehorses, are exempt.</p>
          <p>(b) Materials, including feed and bedding, used or consumed in the breeding, raising, owning, boarding, and keeping of horses are exempt. Machinery, equipment, implements, tools, appliances, furniture, and fixtures used in the breeding, raising, owning, boarding, and keeping of horses are not included within this exemption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.69.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Feed for poultry raised for human consumption.</span></h2>
          <p>Poultry feed is exempt if the poultry is raised for human consumption.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.69.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.13.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 13</a>,44 subd 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/490/#laws.8.4.0">2000 c 490 art 8 s 4</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.55.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.6.12.0">2003 c 127 art 6 s 12</a>-14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/212/#laws.1.22.0">2006 c 212 art 1 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.12.32.0">2008 c 154 art 12 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.70">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.70 EXEMPTIONS FOR GOVERNMENTS AND NONPROFIT GROUPS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To the extent provided in this section, the gross receipts from sales of items to or by, and storage, distribution, use, or consumption of items by the organizations or units of local government listed in this section are specifically exempted from the taxes imposed by this chapter.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary enacted before 1992, only sales to governments and political subdivisions listed in this section are exempt from the taxes imposed by this chapter.</p>
          <p>(c) "Sales" includes purchases under an installment contract or lease purchase agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/465.71">465.71</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Sales to government.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All sales, except those listed in paragraph (b), to the following governments and political subdivisions, or to the listed agencies or instrumentalities of governments and political subdivisions, are exempt:</p>
          <p>(1) the United States and its agencies and instrumentalities;</p>
          <p>(2) school districts, local governments, the University of Minnesota, state universities, community colleges, technical colleges, state academies, the Perpich Minnesota Center for Arts Education, and an instrumentality of a political subdivision that is accredited as an optional/special function school by the North Central Association of Colleges and Schools;</p>
          <p>(3) hospitals and nursing homes owned and operated by political subdivisions of the state of tangible personal property and taxable services used at or by hospitals and nursing homes;</p>
          <p>(4) other states or political subdivisions of other states, if the sale would be exempt from taxation if it occurred in that state; and</p>
          <p>(5) public libraries, public library systems, multicounty, multitype library systems as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/134.001">134.001</a>, county law libraries under chapter 134A, state agency libraries, the state library under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/480.09">480.09</a>, and the Legislative Reference Library.</p>
          <p>(b) This exemption does not apply to the sales of the following products and services:</p>
          <p>(1) building, construction, or reconstruction materials purchased by a contractor or a subcontractor as a part of a lump-sum contract or similar type of contract with a guaranteed maximum price covering both labor and materials for use in the construction, alteration, or repair of a building or facility;</p>
          <p>(2) construction materials purchased by tax exempt entities or their contractors to be used in constructing buildings or facilities which will not be used principally by the tax exempt entities;</p>
          <p>(3) the leasing of a motor vehicle as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297B.01#stat.297B.01.11">297B.01, subdivision 11</a>, except for leases entered into by the United States or its agencies or instrumentalities;</p>
          <p>(4) lodging as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.61#stat.297A.61.3">297A.61, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (g), clause (2), prepared food, candy, soft drinks, alcoholic beverages as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.67#stat.297A.67.2">297A.67, subdivision 2</a>, and taxable cannabis products as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/295.81">295.81, subdivision </a>1, paragraph (r), except for lodging, prepared food, candy, soft drinks, alcoholic beverages, and taxable cannabis products purchased directly by the United States or its agencies or instrumentalities; or</p>
          <p>(5) goods or services purchased by a local government as inputs to a liquor store, taxable cannabis product retailer as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/295.81#stat.295.81.1">295.81, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (p), gas or electric utility, solid waste hauling service, solid waste recycling service, landfill, golf course, marina, campground, cafe, or laundromat.</p>
          <p>(c) As used in this subdivision, "school districts" means public school entities and districts of every kind and nature organized under the laws of the state of Minnesota, and any instrumentality of a school district, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) For purposes of the exemption granted under this subdivision, "local governments" has the following meaning:</p>
          <p>(1) for the period prior to January 1, 2017, local governments means statutory or home rule charter cities, counties, and townships; and</p>
          <p>(2) beginning January 1, 2017, local governments means statutory or home rule charter cities, counties, and townships; special districts as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/6.465">6.465</a>; any instrumentality of a statutory or home rule charter city, county, or township as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>; and any joint powers board or organization created under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Sales of certain goods and services to government.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The following sales to or use by the specified governments and political subdivisions of the state are exempt:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) repair and replacement parts for emergency rescue vehicles, fire trucks, and fire apparatus to a political subdivision;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) machinery and equipment, except for motor vehicles, used directly for mixed municipal solid waste management services at a solid waste disposal facility as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/115A.03#stat.115A.03.10">115A.03, subdivision 10</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) chore and homemaking services to a political subdivision of the state to be provided to elderly individuals or persons with a disability;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) telephone services to the Department of Information Technology Services that are used to provide telecommunications services through the MNIT services revolving fund;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) firefighter personal protective equipment as defined in paragraph (b), if purchased or authorized by and for the use of an organized fire department, fire protection district, or fire company regularly charged with the responsibility of providing fire protection to the state or a political subdivision;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) bullet-resistant body armor that provides the wearer with ballistic and trauma protection, if purchased by a law enforcement agency of the state or a political subdivision of the state, or a licensed peace officer, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.84#stat.626.84.1">626.84, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) motor vehicles purchased or leased by political subdivisions of the state if the vehicles are exempt from registration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.012#stat.168.012.1">168.012, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), exempt from taxation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.448">473.448</a>, or exempt from the motor vehicle sales tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297B.03">297B.03</a>, clause (12);</p>
          <p class="in">(8) equipment designed to process, dewater, and recycle biosolids for wastewater treatment facilities of political subdivisions, and materials incidental to installation of that equipment;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) the removal of trees, bushes, or shrubs for the construction and maintenance of roads, trails, or firebreaks when purchased by an agency of the state or a political subdivision of the state;</p>
          <p class="in">(10) purchases by the Metropolitan Council or the Department of Transportation of vehicles and repair parts to equip operations provided for in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.90">174.90</a>, including, but not limited to, the Northstar Corridor Rail project; and</p>
          <p>(11) purchases of water used directly in providing public safety services by an organized fire department, fire protection district, or fire company regularly charged with the responsibility of providing fire protection to the state or a political subdivision.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) For purposes of this subdivision, "firefighters personal protective equipment" means helmets, including face shields, chin straps, and neck liners; bunker coats and pants, including pant suspenders; boots; gloves; head covers or hoods; wildfire jackets; protective coveralls; goggles; self-contained breathing apparatus; canister filter masks; personal alert safety systems; spanner belts; optical or thermal imaging search devices; and all safety equipment required by the Occupational Safety and Health Administration.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For purchases of items listed in paragraph (a), clause (10), the tax must be imposed and collected as if the rate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62#stat.297A.62.1">297A.62, subdivision 1</a>, applied and then refunded in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.75">297A.75</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Sales to nonprofit groups.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All sales, except those listed in paragraph (b), to the following "nonprofit organizations" are exempt:</p>
          <p>(1) a corporation, society, association, foundation, or institution organized and operated exclusively for charitable, religious, or educational purposes if the item purchased is used in the performance of charitable, religious, or educational functions;</p>
          <p>(2) any senior citizen group or association of groups that:</p>
          <p>(i) in general limits membership to persons who are either age 55 or older, or persons with a physical disability;</p>
          <p>(ii) is organized and operated exclusively for pleasure, recreation, and other nonprofit purposes, not including housing, no part of the net earnings of which inures to the benefit of any private shareholders; and</p>
          <p>(iii) is an exempt organization under section 501(c) of the Internal Revenue Code; and</p>
          <p>(3) an organization that qualifies for an exemption for memberships under subdivision 12 if the item is purchased and used in the performance of the organization's mission.</p>
          <p class="bl">For purposes of this subdivision, charitable purpose includes the maintenance of a cemetery owned by a religious organization.</p>
          <p>(b) This exemption does not apply to the following sales:</p>
          <p>(1) building, construction, or reconstruction materials purchased by a contractor or a subcontractor as a part of a lump-sum contract or similar type of contract with a guaranteed maximum price covering both labor and materials for use in the construction, alteration, or repair of a building or facility;</p>
          <p>(2) construction materials purchased by tax-exempt entities or their contractors to be used in constructing buildings or facilities that will not be used principally by the tax-exempt entities;</p>
          <p>(3) lodging as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.61#stat.297A.61.3">297A.61, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (g), clause (2), prepared food, candy, soft drinks, taxable cannabis products as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/295.81#stat.295.81.1">295.81, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (r), and alcoholic beverages as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.67#stat.297A.67.2">297A.67, subdivision 2</a>, except wine purchased by an established religious organization for sacramental purposes or as allowed under subdivision 9a; and</p>
          <p>(4) leasing of a motor vehicle as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297B.01#stat.297B.01.11">297B.01, subdivision 11</a>, except as provided in paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(c) This exemption applies to the leasing of a motor vehicle as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297B.01#stat.297B.01.11">297B.01, subdivision 11</a>, only if the vehicle is:</p>
          <p>(1) a truck, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.002">168.002</a>, a bus, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.002">168.002</a>, or a passenger automobile, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.002">168.002</a>, if the automobile is designed and used for carrying more than nine persons including the driver; and</p>
          <p>(2) intended to be used primarily to transport tangible personal property or individuals, other than employees, to whom the organization provides service in performing its charitable, religious, or educational purpose.</p>
          <p>(d) A limited liability company also qualifies for exemption under this subdivision if (1) it consists of a sole member that would qualify for the exemption, and (2) the items purchased qualify for the exemption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Veterans groups.</span></h2>
          <p>Sales to an organization of military service veterans or an auxiliary unit of an organization of military service veterans are exempt if:</p>
          <p>(1) the organization or auxiliary unit is organized within the state of Minnesota and is exempt from federal taxation under section 501(c), clause (19), of the Internal Revenue Code; and</p>
          <p>(2) the tangible personal property or services are for charitable, civic, educational, or nonprofit uses and not for social, recreational, pleasure, or profit uses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Ambulances.</span></h2>
          <p>The lease of a motor vehicle by an ambulance service licensed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144E.10">144E.10</a> that is equipped and specifically intended for emergency response or for providing ambulance services is exempt.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Hospitals, outpatient surgical centers, critical access dental providers, and blood centers.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Sales, except for those listed in paragraph (f), to a hospital are exempt, if the items purchased are used in providing hospital services. For purposes of this subdivision, "hospital" means a hospital organized and operated for charitable purposes within the meaning of section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code, and licensed under chapter 144 or by any other jurisdiction, and "hospital services" are services authorized or required to be performed by a "hospital" under chapter 144.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Sales, except for those listed in paragraph (f), to an outpatient surgical center are exempt, if the items purchased are used in providing outpatient surgical services. For purposes of this subdivision, "outpatient surgical center" means an outpatient surgical center organized and operated for charitable purposes within the meaning of section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code, and licensed under chapter 144 or by any other jurisdiction. For the purposes of this subdivision, "outpatient surgical services" means: (1) services authorized or required to be performed by an outpatient surgical center under chapter 144; and (2) urgent care. For purposes of this subdivision, "urgent care" means health services furnished to a person whose medical condition is sufficiently acute to require treatment unavailable through, or inappropriate to be provided by, a clinic or physician's office, but not so acute as to require treatment in a hospital emergency room.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Sales, except for those listed in paragraph (f), to a critical access dental provider are exempt, if the items purchased are used in providing critical access dental care services. For the purposes of this subdivision, "critical access dental provider" means a dentist or dental clinic that qualifies under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.76#stat.256B.76.4">256B.76, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (b), and, in the previous calendar year, had no more than 15 percent of its patients covered by private dental insurance.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Sales, except for those listed in paragraph (f), to a blood center are exempt, if the items purchased are used in providing blood collection and distribution services. Notwithstanding paragraph (f), leases by a blood center of a truck, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.002">168.002</a>; a bus, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.002">168.002</a>; or a passenger automobile, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.002">168.002</a>, if the truck, bus, or automobile is used for carrying out the purposes of the blood center, including the collection of blood from donors, setting up of blood drives, and delivering blood to hospitals are exempt. For purposes of this subdivision, "blood center" means an entity organized and operated for charitable purposes under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code that is:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) registered as a blood establishment pursuant to Code of Federal Regulations, title 21, part 607;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a human cells, tissues, and cellular and tissue-based products establishment under Code of Federal Regulations, title 21, part 1271, subpart B; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a clinical lab that performs infectious disease testing, blood typing, and other laboratory testing services in connection with blood processing for transfusion into humans under Code of Federal Regulations, title 42, part 493.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The exemption provided under paragraph (d) expires January 1, 2028.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) This exemption does not apply to the following products and services:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) purchases made by a clinic, physician's office, or any other medical facility not operating as a hospital, outpatient surgical center, critical access dental provider, or blood center, even though the clinic, office, or facility may be owned and operated by a hospital, outpatient surgical center, critical access dental provider, or blood center;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) sales under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.61#stat.297A.61.3">297A.61, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (g), clause (2), and prepared food, candy, and soft drinks;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) building and construction materials used in constructing buildings or facilities that will not be used principally by the hospital, outpatient surgical center, critical access dental provider, or blood center;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) building, construction, or reconstruction materials purchased by a contractor or a subcontractor as a part of a lump-sum contract or similar type of contract with a guaranteed maximum price covering both labor and materials for use in the construction, alteration, or repair of a hospital, outpatient surgical center, critical access dental provider, or blood center; or</p>
          <p class="in">(5) the leasing of a motor vehicle as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297B.01#stat.297B.01.11">297B.01, subdivision 11</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) A limited liability company also qualifies for exemption under this subdivision if (1) it consists of a sole member that would qualify for the exemption, and (2) the items purchased qualify for the exemption.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) An entity that contains both a hospital and a nonprofit unit may claim this exemption on purchases made for both the hospital and nonprofit unit provided that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the nonprofit unit would have qualified for exemption under subdivision 4; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the items purchased would have qualified for the exemption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Regionwide public safety radio communication system; products and services.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Products and services including, but not limited to, end user equipment used for construction, ownership, operation, maintenance, and enhancement of the backbone system of the regionwide public safety radio communication system established under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.21">403.21</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.40">403.40</a>, are exempt. For purposes of this subdivision, backbone system is defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.21#stat.403.21.9">403.21, subdivision 9</a>. This subdivision is effective for purchases, sales, storage, use, or consumption for use in the first and second phases of the system, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.21">403.21, subdivisions 3, 10, and 11</a>, that portion of the third phase of the system that is located in the southeast district of the State Patrol and the counties of Benton, Sherburne, Stearns, and Wright, and that portion of the system that is located in Itasca County.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Sacramental wine.</span></h2>
          <p>Wine for sacramental purposes in religious ceremonies, as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/340A.316">340A.316</a>, is exempt if the wine is purchased from a nonprofit religious organization meeting the requirements of subdivision 4 or from a person authorized to import sacramental wine without a license as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/340A.316">340A.316</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.9a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9a.<span class="headnote">Established religious orders.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Sales of lodging, prepared food, candy, soft drinks, and alcoholic beverages at noncatered events between an established religious order and an affiliated institution of higher education are exempt.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this subdivision, "established religious order" means an organization directly or indirectly under the control or supervision of a church or convention or association of churches, where members of the organization:</p>
          <p>(1) normally live together as part of a community;</p>
          <p>(2) make long-term commitments to live under a strict set of moral and spiritual rules; and</p>
          <p>(3) work or engage full time in a combination of prayer, religious study, church reform or renewal, or other religious, educational, or charitable goals of the organization.</p>
          <p>(c) For purposes of this subdivision, an institution of higher education is "affiliated" with an established religious order if members of the religious order are represented on the governing board of the institution of higher education and the two organizations share campus space and common facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Nonprofit tickets or admissions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Tickets or admissions to an event are exempt if all the gross receipts are recorded as such, in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles, on the books of one or more organizations whose primary mission is to provide an opportunity for citizens of the state to participate in the creation, performance, or appreciation of the arts, and provided that each organization is:</p>
          <p>(1) an organization described in section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code in which voluntary contributions make up at least five percent of the organization's annual revenue in its most recently completed 12-month fiscal year, or in the current year if the organization has not completed a 12-month fiscal year;</p>
          <p>(2) a municipal board that promotes cultural and arts activities; or</p>
          <p>(3) the University of Minnesota, a state college and university, or a private nonprofit college or university provided that the event is held at a facility owned by the educational institution holding the event.</p>
          <p class="bl">The exemption only applies if the entire proceeds, after reasonable expenses, are used solely to provide opportunities for citizens of the state to participate in the creation, performance, or appreciation of the arts.</p>
          <p>(b) Tickets or admissions to the premises of the Minnesota Zoological Garden are exempt, provided that the exemption under this paragraph does not apply to tickets or admissions to performances or events held on the premises unless the performance or event is sponsored and conducted exclusively by the Minnesota Zoological Board or employees of the Minnesota Zoological Garden.</p>
          <p>(c) Tickets or admissions to a performance or event on the premises of a tax-exempt organization under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code are exempt if:</p>
          <p>(1) the nonprofit organization was established to preserve Minnesota's rural agricultural heritage and focuses on educating the public about rural history and how farms in Minnesota helped to provide food for the nation and the world;</p>
          <p>(2) the premises of the nonprofit organization is at least 115 acres;</p>
          <p>(3) the performance or event is sponsored and conducted exclusively by volunteers, employees of the nonprofit organization, or members of the board of directors of the nonprofit organization; and</p>
          <p>(4) the performance or event is consistent with the nonprofit organization's purposes under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">School tickets or admissions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Tickets or admissions to regular season school games, events, and activities are exempt. For purposes of this subdivision, "school" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22#stat.120A.22.4">120A.22, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.11a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11a.<span class="headnote">Minnesota State High School League tickets and admissions.</span></h2>
          <p>Tickets and admissions to games, events, and activities sponsored by the Minnesota State High School League under chapter 128C are exempt.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">YMCA, YWCA, JCC, and similar memberships.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The sale of memberships, meaning both onetime initiation fees and periodic membership dues, to an association incorporated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/315.44">315.44</a> or an organization defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/315.51">315.51</a>, or a nonprofit organization offering similar services are exempt. However, all separate charges made for the privilege of having access to and the use of the association's sports and athletic facilities are taxable.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this subdivision, a "nonprofit organization offering similar services" means an exempt organization under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code whose mission is to support youth and families through a variety of activities, including membership allowing access to athletic facilities, and who provides free or reduced-price memberships to seniors or low-income persons or families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Fundraising sales by or for nonprofit groups.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The following sales by the specified organizations for fundraising purposes are exempt, subject to the limitations listed in paragraph (b):</p>
          <p>(1) all sales made by a nonprofit organization that exists solely for the purpose of providing educational or social activities for young people primarily age 18 and under;</p>
          <p>(2) all sales made by an organization that is a senior citizen group or association of groups if (i) in general it limits membership to persons age 55 or older; (ii) it is organized and operated exclusively for pleasure, recreation, and other nonprofit purposes; and (iii) no part of its net earnings inures to the benefit of any private shareholders;</p>
          <p>(3) the sale or use of tickets or admissions to a golf tournament held in Minnesota if the beneficiary of the tournament's net proceeds qualifies as a tax-exempt organization under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code; and</p>
          <p>(4) sales of candy sold for fundraising purposes by a nonprofit organization that provides educational and social activities primarily for young people age 18 and under.</p>
          <p>(b) The exemptions listed in paragraph (a) are limited in the following manner:</p>
          <p>(1) the exemption under paragraph (a), clauses (1) and (2), applies only to the first $20,000 of the gross annual receipts of the organization from fundraising;</p>
          <p>(2) the exemption under paragraph (a), clause (1), does not apply if the sales are derived from admission charges or from activities for which the money must be deposited with the school district treasurer under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.49#stat.123B.49.2">123B.49, subdivision 2</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) the exemption under paragraph (a), clause (1), does not apply if the sales are derived from admission charges or from activities for which the money must be recorded in the same manner as other revenues or expenditures of the school district under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.49#stat.123B.49.4">123B.49, subdivision 4</a>, unless the following conditions are both met:</p>
          <p>(i) the sales are made for fundraising purposes of a club, association, or other organization of elementary or secondary school students organized for the purpose of carrying on sports activities, educational activities, or other extracurricular activities; and</p>
          <p>(ii) the school district reserves revenue raised for extracurricular activities, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.49#stat.123B.49.4">123B.49, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (e), and spends the revenue raised by a particular extracurricular activity only for that extracurricular activity.</p>
          <p>(c) Sales of tangible personal property and services are exempt if the entire proceeds, less the necessary expenses for obtaining the property or services, will be contributed to a registered combined charitable organization described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.50">43A.50</a>, to be used exclusively for charitable, religious, or educational purposes, and the registered combined charitable organization has given its written permission for the sale. Sales that occur over a period of more than 24 days per year are not exempt under this paragraph.</p>
          <p>(d) For purposes of this subdivision, a club, association, or other organization of elementary or secondary school students organized for the purpose of carrying on sports, educational, or other extracurricular activities is a separate organization from the school district or school for purposes of applying the $20,000 limit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Fundraising events sponsored by nonprofit groups.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Sales of tangible personal property or services at, and admission charges for fundraising events sponsored by, a nonprofit organization are exempt if:</p>
          <p>(1) all gross receipts are recorded as such, in accordance with generally accepted accounting practices, on the books of the nonprofit organization; and</p>
          <p>(2) the entire proceeds, less the necessary expenses for the event, will be used solely and exclusively for charitable, religious, or educational purposes. Exempt sales include the sale of prepared food, candy, and soft drinks at the fundraising event.</p>
          <p>(b) This exemption is limited in the following manner:</p>
          <p>(1) it does not apply to admission charges for events involving bingo or other gambling activities or to charges for use of amusement devices involving bingo or other gambling activities;</p>
          <p>(2) all gross receipts are taxable if the profits are not used solely and exclusively for charitable, religious, or educational purposes;</p>
          <p>(3) it does not apply unless the organization keeps a separate accounting record, including receipts and disbursements from each fundraising event that documents all deductions from gross receipts with receipts and other records;</p>
          <p>(4) it does not apply to any sale made by or in the name of a nonprofit corporation as the active or passive agent of a person that is not a nonprofit corporation;</p>
          <p>(5) all gross receipts are taxable if fundraising events exceed 24 days per year;</p>
          <p>(6) it does not apply to fundraising events conducted on premises leased for more than ten days but less than 30 days; and</p>
          <p>(7) it does not apply if the risk of the event is not borne by the nonprofit organization and the benefit to the nonprofit organization is less than the total amount of the state and local tax revenues forgone by this exemption.</p>
          <p>(c) For purposes of this subdivision, a "nonprofit organization" means any unit of government, corporation, society, association, foundation, or institution organized and operated for charitable, religious, educational, civic, fraternal, and senior citizens' or veterans' purposes, no part of the net earnings of which inures to the benefit of a private individual.</p>
          <p>(d) For purposes of this subdivision, "fundraising events" means activities of limited duration, not regularly carried out in the normal course of business, that attract patrons for community, social, and entertainment purposes, such as auctions, bake sales, ice cream socials, block parties, carnivals, competitions, concerts, concession stands, craft sales, bazaars, dinners, dances, door-to-door sales of merchandise, fairs, fashion shows, festivals, galas, special event workshops, sporting activities such as marathons and tournaments, and similar events. Fundraising events do not include the operation of a regular place of business in which services are provided or sales are made during regular hours such as bookstores, thrift stores, gift shops, restaurants, ongoing Internet sales, regularly scheduled classes, or other activities carried out in the normal course of business.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Statewide amateur athletic games.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.61#stat.297A.61.3">297A.61, subdivision 3</a>, or any other provision of this chapter, the gross receipts from the following sales made to or by a nonprofit corporation designated by the Minnesota Amateur Sports Commission to conduct a series of statewide amateur athletic games and related events, workshops, and clinics are exempt:</p>
          <p>(1) sales of tangible personal property to or the storage, use, or other consumption of tangible personal property by the nonprofit corporation; and</p>
          <p>(2) sales of tangible personal property, admission charges, and sales of prepared food, candy, and soft drinks by the nonprofit corporation at fundraising events, athletic events, or athletic facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Camp fees.</span></h2>
          <p>Fees to camps or other recreation facilities are exempt for:</p>
          <p>(1) services primarily for children, adults accompanying children, or persons with a disability; or</p>
          <p>(2) educational or religious activities;</p>
          <p class="bl">if the camp or facilities are owned and operated by an exempt organization under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Private communication service for State Lottery.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Private communication service, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.61#stat.297A.61.26">297A.61, subdivision 26</a>, is exempt if the service is purchased by an agent acting on behalf of the State Lottery.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Nursing homes and boarding care homes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All sales, except those listed in paragraph (b), to a nursing home licensed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144A.02">144A.02</a> or a boarding care home certified as a nursing facility under title 19 of the Social Security Act are exempt if the facility:</p>
          <p>(1) is exempt from federal income taxation pursuant to section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code; and</p>
          <p>(2) is certified to participate in the medical assistance program under title 19 of the Social Security Act, or certifies to the commissioner that it does not discharge residents due to the inability to pay.</p>
          <p>(b) This exemption does not apply to the following sales:</p>
          <p>(1) building, construction, or reconstruction materials purchased by a contractor or a subcontractor as a part of a lump-sum contract or similar type of contract with a guaranteed maximum price covering both labor and materials for use in the construction, alteration, or repair of a building or facility;</p>
          <p>(2) construction materials purchased by tax-exempt entities or their contractors to be used in constructing buildings or facilities that will not be used principally by the tax-exempt entities;</p>
          <p>(3) lodging as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.61#stat.297A.61.3">297A.61, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (g), clause (2), prepared food, candy, soft drinks, and alcoholic beverages as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.67#stat.297A.67.2">297A.67, subdivision 2</a>, and taxable cannabis products as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/295.81">295.81, subdivision </a>1, paragraph (r); and</p>
          <p>(4) leasing of a motor vehicle as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297B.01#stat.297B.01.11">297B.01, subdivision 11</a>, except as provided in paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(c) This exemption applies to the leasing of a motor vehicle as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297B.01#stat.297B.01.11">297B.01, subdivision 11</a>, only if the vehicle is:</p>
          <p>(1) a truck, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.002">168.002</a>; a bus, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.002">168.002</a>; or a passenger automobile, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.002">168.002</a>, if the automobile is designed and used for carrying more than nine persons including the driver; and</p>
          <p>(2) intended to be used primarily to transport tangible personal property or residents of the nursing home or boarding care home.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Nonprofit snowmobile clubs; machinery and equipment.</span></h2>
          <p>Sales of tangible personal property to a nonprofit snowmobile club that is used primarily and directly for the grooming of state or grant-in-aid snowmobile trails are exempt. The exemption applies to grooming machines, attachments, other associated accessories, and repair parts. A nonprofit snowmobile club is eligible for the exemption under this subdivision if it received, in the current year or in the previous three-year period, a state grant-in-aid maintenance and grooming grant administered by the Department of Natural Resources by applying for the grant with a local unit of government sponsor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.<span class="headnote">Ice arenas and rinks.</span></h2>
          <p>Sales to organizations that exist primarily for the purpose of owning or operating ice arenas or rinks that are (1) part of either the Duluth Heritage Sports Center or the David M. Thaler Sports Center; and (2) are used for youth and high school programs, are exempt if the organization is a private, nonprofit corporation exempt from federal income taxation under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.70.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.<span class="headnote">County agricultural society sales at county fairs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The following sales by a county agricultural society are exempt:</p>
          <p>(1) admissions to and parking at the county fairgrounds;</p>
          <p>(2) admissions to separately ticketed events run by the county agricultural society; and</p>
          <p>(3) concessions and other sales made by employees or volunteers of the county agricultural society on the county fairgrounds.</p>
          <p>(b) The exemption under paragraph (a) does not apply to sales for events held at a time other than at the time of the regularly scheduled county fair, or events not held on the county fairgrounds.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.70.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.14.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 14</a>,44 subd 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/490/#laws.8.9.0">2000 c 490 art 8 s 9</a>-11;       </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>Subdivision 11a, as added by Laws 2017, First Special Session chapter 1, article 3, section 26, is effective for sales and purchases made after June 30, 2017, and before July 1, 2027. Laws 2017, First Special Session chapter 1, article 3, section 26, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.71">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.71 CONSTRUCTION EXEMPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The gross receipts from the sale of, and storage, distribution, use, or consumption of the tangible personal property contained in this section are specifically exempted from the taxes imposed by this chapter. Building materials, equipment, and supplies and other items exempt under this section are exempt regardless of whether purchased by the owner or a contractor, subcontractor, or builder.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.95.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 95</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Correctional facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>Building materials and supplies for constructing or improving an adult or juvenile correctional facility by a county, home rule charter city, or statutory city are exempt if the project is mandated by state or federal law, rule, or regulation. The tax must be imposed and collected as if the rate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62#stat.297A.62.1">297A.62, subdivision 1</a>, applied and then refunded in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.75">297A.75</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Business incubator and industrial park.</span></h2>
          <p>Building materials and supplies for construction of a facility that includes a business incubator and industrial park are exempt if the facility:</p>
          <p>(1) is owned and operated by a nonprofit charitable organization that qualifies for tax exemption under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code;</p>
          <p>(2) is used for the development of nonretail businesses, offering access to equipment, space, services, and advice to the tenant businesses, for the purpose of encouraging economic development and job creation in the area served by the organization, and emphasizes development of businesses that manufacture products from materials found in the waste stream, or manufacture alternative energy and conservation systems, or make use of emerging environmental technologies;</p>
          <p>(3) includes in its structure systems of material and energy exchanges that use waste products from one industrial process as sources of energy and material for other processes; and</p>
          <p>(4) makes use of solar and wind energy technology and incorporates salvaged materials in its construction.</p>
          <p>A limited liability company also qualifies for exemption under this subdivision if (1) it consists of a sole member that would qualify for the exemption, and (2) the items purchased qualify for the exemption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Wood waste cogeneration facility.</span></h2>
          <p>Building materials and supplies for constructing, equipping, or modifying a district heating and cooling system cogeneration facility are exempt if the facility:</p>
          <p>(1) utilizes wood waste as a primary fuel source; and</p>
          <p>(2) satisfies the requirements of the biomass mandate in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2424#stat.216B.2424.5">216B.2424, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Building materials; disabled veterans.</span></h2>
          <p>Building materials to be used in the construction or remodeling of a residence are exempt when the construction or remodeling is financed in whole or in part by the United States in accordance with United States Code, title 38, sections 2101 to 2105. The tax must be imposed and collected as if the rate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62#stat.297A.62.1">297A.62, subdivision 1</a>, applied and then refunded in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.75">297A.75</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Chair lifts, ramps, elevators.</span></h2>
          <p>Elevators and building materials used to install or construct chair lifts, ramps, and elevators are exempt, if they are authorized by a physician and installed in or attached to the owner's homestead. The tax must be imposed and collected as if the rate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62#stat.297A.62.1">297A.62, subdivision 1</a>, applied and then refunded in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.75">297A.75</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Agriculture processing facility materials.</span></h2>
          <p>Building materials and supplies for constructing an agriculture processing facility as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/469.1811">469.1811</a> in which the total capital investment in the processing facility is expected to exceed $100,000,000 are exempt. The tax must be imposed and collected as if the rate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62#stat.297A.62.1">297A.62, subdivision 1</a>, applied, and then refunded in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.75">297A.75</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Mineral production facilities.</span></h2>
          <p>Building materials, equipment, and supplies used for the construction of the following mineral production facilities are exempt.</p>
          <p>The mineral production facilities that qualify for this exemption are:</p>
          <p>(1) a value added iron products plant, which may be either a new plant or a facility incorporated into an existing plant that produces iron upgraded to a minimum of 75 percent iron content or any iron alloy with a total minimum metallic content of 90 percent;</p>
          <p>(2) a facility used for the manufacture of fluxed taconite pellets as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.24">298.24</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) a new capital project that has a total cost of over $40,000,000 that is directly related to production, cost, or quality at an existing taconite facility that does not qualify under clause (1) or (2); and</p>
          <p>(4) a new mine or minerals processing plant for any mineral subject to the gross proceeds tax imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.015">298.015</a>.</p>
          <p>The tax must be imposed and collected as if the rate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62#stat.297A.62.1">297A.62, subdivision 1</a>, applied, and then refunded in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.75">297A.75</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.95.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 95</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.9.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/377/#laws.3.24.0">2002 c 377 art 3 s 24</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.</h2>
          <p>MS 2006 [Expired]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.94.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 94</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.</h2>
          <p>MS 2004 [Expired]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Materials used to make residential property accessible to persons with a disability.</span></h2>
          <p>Building materials and equipment sold to, or stored, used, or consumed by, a nonprofit organization are exempt if:</p>
          <p>(1) the materials and equipment are used or incorporated into modifying an existing residential structure to make it accessible to persons with a disability; and</p>
          <p>(2) the materials and equipment used in the modification would qualify for an exemption under either subdivision 11 or 12 if made by the current owner of the residence.</p>
          <p>For purposes of this subdivision, "nonprofit organization" means any nonprofit corporation, society, association, foundation, or institution organized and operated exclusively for charitable, religious, educational, or civic purposes; or a veterans' group exempt from federal taxation under section 501(c), clause (19), of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.<span class="headnote">Construction materials for qualified low-income housing projects.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Purchases of materials and supplies used or consumed in and equipment incorporated into the construction, improvement, or expansion of qualified low-income housing projects are exempt from the tax imposed under this chapter if the owner of the qualified low-income housing project is:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the public housing agency or housing and redevelopment authority of a political subdivision;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) an entity exercising the powers of a housing and redevelopment authority within a political subdivision;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a limited partnership in which the sole or managing general partner is an authority under clause (1) or an entity under clause (2), (4), or (5);</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a nonprofit corporation subject to the provisions of chapter 317A, and qualifying under section 501(c)(3) or 501(c)(4) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) a limited liability company if it consists of a sole member that is an entity under clause (4); or</p>
          <p>(6) an owner entity, as defined in Code of Federal Regulations, title 24, part 941.604, for a qualified low-income housing project described in paragraph (b), clause (5).</p>
          <p class="in">This exemption applies regardless of whether the purchases are made by the owner of the facility or a contractor.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) For purposes of this exemption, "qualified low-income housing project" means:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a housing or mixed use project in which at least 20 percent of the residential units are qualifying low-income rental housing units as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.128">273.128</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a federally assisted low-income housing project financed by a mortgage insured or held by the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development under United States Code, title 12, section 1701s, 1715l(d)(3), 1715l(d)(4), or 1715z-1; United States Code, title 42, section 1437f; the Native American Housing Assistance and Self-Determination Act, United States Code, title 25, section 4101 et seq.; or any similar successor federal low-income housing program;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a qualified low-income housing project as defined in United States Code, title 26, section 42(g), meeting all of the requirements for a low-income housing credit under section 42 of the Internal Revenue Code regardless of whether the project actually applies for or receives a low-income housing credit;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a project that will be operated in compliance with Internal Revenue Service revenue procedure 96-32; or</p>
          <p class="in">(5) a housing or mixed use project in which all or a portion of the residential units are subject to the requirements of section 5 of the United States Housing Act of 1937.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For a project, a portion of which is not used for low-income housing units, the amount of purchases that are exempt under this subdivision must be determined by multiplying the total purchases, as specified in paragraph (a), by the ratio of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the total gross square footage of units subject to the income limits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.128">273.128</a>, the financing for the project, the federal low-income housing tax credit, revenue procedure 96-32, or section 5 of the United States Housing Act of 1937, as applicable to the project; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the total gross square footage of all units in the project.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The tax must be imposed and collected as if the rate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62#stat.297A.62.1">297A.62, subdivision 1</a>, applied, and then refunded in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.75">297A.75</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.24">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24.</h2>
          <p>[Expired]</p>
        </div>

          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25.</h2>
          <p>MS 2006 [Expired]</p>
        </div>

          <p>[Expired]</p>
        </div>

          <p>[Expired]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.71.28">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28.</h2>
          <p>[Expired]</p>

          <p>[Expired]</p>
        </div>
