          <p class="in">(c) The total cost of such equipment and parts and accessories attached or to be attached to such equipment must be multiplied by a fraction. The numerator of the fraction is the Minnesota mileage as reported on the current pro rata application provided for in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/168.187">168.187</a> and the denominator of the fraction is the total mileage reported on the current pro rata registration application. The amount so determined must be multiplied by the tax rate to obtain the tax due.</p>
          <p class="bl">In computing the tax under this section "sales price" does not include the amount of any taxes imposed directly on the consumer that are separately stated on the invoice, bill of sale, or similar document given to the purchaser.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A retailer covered by this section shall make a return and remit to the commissioner the tax due for the preceding calendar month in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.11">289A.11</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.20#stat.289A.20.4">289A.20, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.90.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Registration subsequent to payment of tax.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who has paid the tax under this chapter or chapter 297B and who meets the requirements of this section at the time of the sale, but was not registered under this section at the time of the sale, may register as a retailer, make a return, and file for a refund of the difference between the tax calculated under this chapter or chapter 297B and the tax calculated under subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.90.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Agreement with commissioner of public safety.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivisions 1 to 3, the commissioner may enter into an agreement with the commissioner of public safety whereby, upon approval of both commissioners, the commissioner of public safety shall collect the sales tax on motor vehicles from persons defined in subdivision 1. For the purpose of collecting the tax, the commissioner of public safety shall act as the agent of the commissioner of revenue and shall be subject to all rules consistent with this chapter that may be prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.90.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.33.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/213/#laws.0.30.0">2001 c 213 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.77.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 77</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.12.37.0">2008 c 154 art 12 s 37</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ENFORCEMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.91">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.91 SEIZURE; COURT REVIEW.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.91.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Seizure of property used in illegal transport.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the retailer does not have a sales or use tax permit and has been engaging in transporting personal property into the state without payment of the tax, the commissioner of revenue or the commissioner's agents may seize in the name of the state any truck, automobile, or means of transportation not owned or operated by a for-hire carrier, used in the illegal importation and transportation of any tangible personal property by a retailer or the retailer's agent or employee. The commissioner may demand the forfeiture and sale of the truck, automobile, or other means of transportation together with the property being transported illegally, unless the owner establishes to the satisfaction of the commissioner or the court that the owner had no notice or knowledge or reason to believe that the vehicle was used or intended to be used in any such violation.</p>
          <p>(b) Within ten days after the seizure, the person making the seizure shall serve by certified mail an inventory of the vehicle and property seized on the person from whom the seizure was made, if known, and on any person known or believed to have any right, title, interest, or lien on the vehicle or property, at the last known address. The person making the seizure shall also file a copy of the inventory with the commissioner. The notice must include an explanation of the right to demand a judicial forfeiture determination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.91.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Court review of forfeiture.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Within 60 days after the date of service of the inventory, which is the date of mailing, the person from whom the vehicle and property were seized or any person claiming an interest in the vehicle or property may file a demand for a judicial determination of the question of whether the vehicle or property was lawfully subject to seizure and forfeiture.</p>
          <p>(b) The demand must be in the form of a civil complaint and must be filed with the court administrator in the county in which the seizure occurred, together with proof of service or a copy of the complaint on the commissioner of revenue, and the standard filing fee for civil actions unless the petitioner has the right to sue in forma pauperis under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/563.01">563.01</a>. If the value of the seized property or vehicle is $15,000 or less, the claimant may file an action in conciliation court for its recovery. If the value of the seized property or vehicle is less than $500, the claimant does not have to pay the conciliation court filing fee.</p>
          <p>(c) The complaint must be captioned in the name of the claimant as plaintiff and the seized property or vehicle as defendant, and must state with specificity the grounds on which the claimant alleges the property or vehicle was improperly seized and the plaintiff's interest in the property or vehicle seized. No responsive pleading is required of the commissioner, and no court fees may be charged for the commissioner's appearance in the matter. The proceedings are governed by the Rules of Civil Procedure. Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, an action for the return of property or a vehicle seized under this subdivision may not be maintained by or on behalf of any person who has been served with an inventory unless the person has complied with this subdivision. The court shall hear the action without a jury and shall determine the issues of fact and law involved. If a judgment of forfeiture is entered and is not stayed pending an appeal, the commissioner may have the forfeited vehicle and property sold at public auction as provided by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.91.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Treatment of seized property.</span></h2>
          <p>If no demand for judicial determination is made, the vehicle and property seized are considered forfeited to the state by operation of law and may be disposed of by the commissioner as if there were a judgment of forfeiture. The forfeiture and sale of the automobile, truck, or other means of transportation, and of the property being transported illegally in it, are a penalty for the violation of this chapter. After deducting the expense of keeping the vehicle and property, the fee for seizure, and the costs of the sale, the commissioner shall pay liens from the funds collected. The commissioner shall pay all liens, according to their priority, that are established as being bona fide and as existing without the lienor having any notice or knowledge that the vehicle or property was being used or was intended to be used for or in connection with any such violation. The commissioner shall pay the balance of the proceeds into the state treasury to be credited to the general fund. The state is not liable for any liens in excess of the proceeds from the sale after allowable deductions. A sale under this section frees the vehicle and property sold from all liens.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.91.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.34.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.78.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 78</a>; art 18 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/283/#laws.0.3.0">2012 c 283 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.1.85.0">2018 c 182 art 1 s 85</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.92">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.92 SECURITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.92.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Amount of security.</span></h2>
          <p>To ensure compliance with the taxes imposed by this chapter, the commissioner may require a retailer subject to this chapter to deposit security with the commissioner. The security must be in the form and amount the commissioner requires, but not more than twice the retailer's estimated average liability for the period for which the returns are required to be filed, or $10,000, whichever is less. The amount of security may be increased or decreased by the commissioner, subject to the limitations in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.92.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Auctions of security.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may sell property deposited as security at public auction if necessary to recover the amount required to be collected, including any interest and penalties. Notice of the sale must be served upon the person who deposited the security. It must be served personally, or by mail as prescribed for an order of assessment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.33#stat.270C.33.8">270C.33, subdivision 8</a>. After a sale any surplus above the amount due not required as security under this section must be returned to the person who deposited the security.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.92.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Bond.</span></h2>
          <p>In lieu of security, the commissioner may require a retailer to file a bond. The bond must be issued by a surety company authorized to transact business in this state and approved by the commissioner of commerce as to solvency and responsibility.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.92.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.35.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.79.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 79</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.17.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.297A.93"><b>297A.93</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">DEPOSIT OF REVENUES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.94">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.94 DEPOSIT OF REVENUES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except as provided in this section, the commissioner shall deposit the revenues, including interest and penalties, derived from the taxes imposed by this chapter in the state treasury and credit them to the general fund.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner shall deposit taxes in the Minnesota agricultural and economic account in the special revenue fund if:</p>
        <p>(1) the taxes are derived from sales and use of property and services purchased for the construction and operation of an agricultural resource project; and</p>
        <p>(2) the purchase was made on or after the date on which a conditional commitment was made for a loan guaranty for the project under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/41A.04#stat.41A.04.3">41A.04, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        <p class="bl">The commissioner of management and budget shall certify to the commissioner the date on which the project received the conditional commitment. The amount deposited in the loan guaranty account must be reduced by any refunds and by the costs incurred by the Department of Revenue to administer and enforce the assessment and collection of the taxes.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner shall deposit the revenues, including interest and penalties, derived from the taxes imposed on sales and purchases included in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.61#stat.297A.61.3">297A.61, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (g), clauses (1) and (4), in the state treasury, and credit them as follows:</p>
        <p>(1) first to the general obligation special tax bond debt service account in each fiscal year the amount required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.661#stat.16A.661.3">16A.661, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (b); and</p>
        <p>(2) after the requirements of clause (1) have been met, the balance to the general fund.</p>
        <p>(d) Beginning with sales taxes remitted after July 1, 2017, the commissioner shall deposit in the state treasury the revenues collected under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.64#stat.297A.64.1">297A.64, subdivision 1</a>, including interest and penalties and minus refunds, and credit them to the highway user tax distribution fund.</p>
        <p>(e) The commissioner shall deposit the revenues, including interest and penalties, collected under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.64#stat.297A.64.5">297A.64, subdivision 5</a>, in the state treasury and credit them to the general fund. By July 15 of each year the commissioner shall transfer to the highway user tax distribution fund an amount equal to the excess fees collected under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.64#stat.297A.64.5">297A.64, subdivision 5</a>, for the previous calendar year.</p>
        <p>(f) Beginning with sales taxes remitted after July 1, 2017, in conjunction with the deposit of revenues under paragraph (d), the commissioner shall deposit into the state treasury and credit to the highway user tax distribution fund an amount equal to the estimated revenues derived from the tax rate imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62#stat.297A.62.1">297A.62, subdivision 1</a>, on the lease or rental for not more than 28 days of rental motor vehicles subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.64">297A.64</a>. The commissioner shall estimate the amount of sales tax revenue deposited under this paragraph based on the amount of revenue deposited under paragraph (d).</p>
        <p>(g) The commissioner must deposit the revenues derived from the taxes imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62">297A.62, subdivision </a>1, on the sale and purchase of motor vehicle repair and replacement parts in the state treasury and credit:</p>
        <p>(1) 43.5 percent in each fiscal year to the highway user tax distribution fund;</p>
        <p>(2) a percentage to the transportation advancement account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.49">174.49</a> as follows:</p>
        <p>(i) 3.5 percent in fiscal year 2024;</p>
        <p>(ii) 4.5 percent in fiscal year 2025;</p>
        <p>(iii) 5.5 percent in fiscal year 2026;</p>
        <p>(iv) 7.5 percent in fiscal year 2027;</p>
        <p>(v) 14.5 percent in fiscal year 2028;</p>
        <p>(vi) 21.5 percent in fiscal year 2029;</p>
        <p>(vii) 28.5 percent in fiscal year 2030;</p>
        <p>(viii) 36.5 percent in fiscal year 2031;</p>
        <p>(ix) 44.5 percent in fiscal year 2032; and</p>
        <p>(x) 56.5 percent in fiscal year 2033 and thereafter; and</p>
        <p>(3) the remainder in each fiscal year to the general fund.</p>
        <p class="bl">For purposes of this paragraph, "motor vehicle" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297B.01#stat.297B.01.11">297B.01, subdivision 11</a>, and "motor vehicle repair and replacement parts" includes (i) all parts, tires, accessories, and equipment incorporated into or affixed to the motor vehicle as part of the motor vehicle maintenance and repair, and (ii) paint, oil, and other fluids that remain on or in the motor vehicle as part of the motor vehicle maintenance or repair. For purposes of this paragraph, "tire" means any tire of the type used on highway vehicles, if wholly or partially made of rubber and if marked according to federal regulations for highway use.</p>
        <p>(h) 81.56 percent of the revenues, including interest and penalties, transmitted to the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.65">297A.65</a>, must be deposited by the commissioner in the state treasury as follows:</p>
        <p>(1) 47.5 percent of the receipts must be deposited in the heritage enhancement account in the game and fish fund, and may be spent only on activities that improve, enhance, or protect fish and wildlife resources, including conservation, restoration, and enhancement of land, water, and other natural resources of the state;</p>
        <p>(2) 22.5 percent of the receipts must be deposited in the natural resources fund, and may be spent only for state parks and trails;</p>
        <p>(3) 22.5 percent of the receipts must be deposited in the natural resources fund, and may be spent only on metropolitan park and trail grants;</p>
        <p>(4) three percent of the receipts must be deposited in the natural resources fund, and may be spent only on local trail grants;</p>
        <p>(5) two percent of the receipts must be deposited in the natural resources fund, and may be spent only for the Minnesota Zoological Garden, the Como Park Zoo and Conservatory, and the Duluth Zoo; and</p>
        <p>(6) 2.5 percent of the receipts must be deposited in the pollinator account established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/103B.101#stat.103B.101.19">103B.101, subdivision 19</a>.</p>
        <p>(i) 1.5 percent of the revenues, including interest and penalties, transmitted to the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.65">297A.65</a> must be deposited in a regional parks and trails account in the natural resources fund and may only be spent for parks and trails of regional significance outside of the seven-county metropolitan area under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.535">85.535</a>, based on recommendations from the Greater Minnesota Regional Parks and Trails Commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/85.536">85.536</a>.</p>
        <p>(j) 1.5 percent of the revenues, including interest and penalties, transmitted to the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.65">297A.65</a> must be deposited in an outdoor recreational opportunities for underserved communities account in the natural resources fund and may only be spent on projects and activities that connect diverse and underserved Minnesotans through expanding cultural environmental experiences, exploration of their environment, and outdoor recreational activities.</p>
        <p>(k) The revenue dedicated under paragraph (h) may not be used as a substitute for traditional sources of funding for the purposes specified, but the dedicated revenue shall supplement traditional sources of funding for those purposes. Land acquired with money deposited in the game and fish fund under paragraph (h) must be open to public hunting and fishing during the open season, except that in aquatic management areas or on lands where angling easements have been acquired, fishing may be prohibited during certain times of the year and hunting may be prohibited. At least 87 percent of the money deposited in the game and fish fund for improvement, enhancement, or protection of fish and wildlife resources under paragraph (h) must be allocated for field operations.</p>
        <p>(l) The commissioner must deposit the revenues, including interest and penalties minus any refunds, derived from the sale of items regulated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/624.20#stat.624.20.1">624.20, subdivision 1</a>, that may be sold to persons 18 years old or older and that are not prohibited from use by the general public under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/624.21">624.21</a>, in the state treasury and credit:</p>
        <p>(1) 25 percent to the volunteer fire assistance grant account established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/88.068">88.068</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) 25 percent to the fire safety account established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297I.06">297I.06, subdivision </a>3; and</p>
        <p>(3) the remainder to the general fund.</p>
        <p>For purposes of this paragraph, the percentage of total sales and use tax revenue derived from the sale of items regulated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/624.20#stat.624.20.1">624.20, subdivision 1</a>, that are allowed to be sold to persons 18 years old or older and are not prohibited from use by the general public under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/624.21">624.21</a>, is a set percentage of the total sales and use tax revenues collected in the state, with the percentage determined under Laws 2017, First Special Session chapter 1, article 3, section 39.</p>
        <p>(m) The revenues deposited under paragraphs (a) to (l) do not include the revenues, including interest and penalties, generated by the sales tax imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.62#stat.297A.62.1a">297A.62, subdivision 1a</a>, which must be deposited as provided under the Minnesota Constitution, article XI, section 15.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.94.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.37.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/488/#laws.3.29.0">2000 c 488 art 3 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/185/#laws.0.33.0">2001 c 185 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/2/#laws.0.146.0">1Sp2001 c 2 s 146</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.80.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/353/#laws.0.2.0">2002 c 353 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/128/#laws.1.154.0">2003 c 128 art 1 s 154</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.4.9.0">2009 c 88 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.76.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 76</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.3.37.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 3 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/3/#laws.3.110.0">1Sp2017 c 3 art 3 s 110</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.142.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 142</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.4.96.0">2023 c 60 art 4 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.3.27.0">2023 c 68 art 3 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/116/#laws.4.6.0">2024 c 116 art 4 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LOCAL SALES AND USE TAXES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.95">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.95 COORDINATION OF STATE AND LOCAL SALES TAX RATES.</h1>
        <p>In preparing and distributing a sales tax schedule for use within a local jurisdiction with a separate general sales tax, the state Department of Revenue shall coordinate the state and local sales tax so that a sale of $1 reflects a tax equal to the combination of the state and local sales tax rates. The combined sales tax on other sales amounts must also reflect the coordinated rather than the separate effects of the state and local sales taxes. The schedule must be coordinated as long as the local sales tax is in effect. If the sales tax percentage is changed for either of the taxes, the schedule must be adjusted to reflect the change.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.95.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.38.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 38</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.96">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.96 LOCAL ADMISSIONS AND AMUSEMENT TAXES; EXEMPTION FOR NONPROFIT ORGANIZATIONS.</h1>
        <p>Amounts charged for admission to an event described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.70#stat.297A.70.10">297A.70, subdivision 10</a>, paragraph (a), are not subject to a tax imposed by a local unit of government or imposed on sales taking place in a single named local unit of government on sales of admissions or amusements, under a law other than a general sales tax law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.96.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.39.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/377/#laws.3.18.0">2002 c 377 art 3 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.297A.97"><b>297A.97</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.6.18.0">2003 c 127 art 6 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.98">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.98 LOCAL GOVERNMENTS EXEMPT FROM LOCAL SALES TAXES.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding any other law, ordinance, or charter provision, a political subdivision of the state is not required to pay any general sales tax imposed by a political subdivision of the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.98.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.41.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 41</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.99">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.99 LOCAL SALES TAXES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization; scope.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A political subdivision of this state may impose a general sales tax (1) under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.9915">297A.9915</a>, (2) under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.992">297A.992</a>, (3) under section 297A.9925, (4) under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.993">297A.993</a>, (5) if permitted by special law, or (6) if the political subdivision enacted and imposed the tax before January 1, 1982, and its predecessor provision.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) This section governs the imposition of a general sales tax by the political subdivision. The provisions of this section preempt the provisions of any special law:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) enacted before June 2, 1997, or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) enacted on or after June 2, 1997, that does not explicitly exempt the special law provision from this section's rules by reference.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) This section does not apply to or preempt a sales tax on motor vehicles. Beginning July 1, 2019, no political subdivision may impose a special excise tax on motor vehicles unless it is imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.993">297A.993</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) A political subdivision may not advertise or expend funds for the promotion of a referendum to support imposing a local sales tax and may only spend funds related to imposing a local sales tax to:</p>
          <p>(1) conduct the referendum;</p>
          <p>(2) disseminate information included in the resolution adopted under subdivision 2, but only if the disseminated information includes a list of specific projects and the cost of each individual project;</p>
          <p>(3) provide notice of, and conduct public forums at which proponents and opponents on the merits of the referendum are given equal time to express their opinions on the merits of the referendum;</p>
          <p>(4) provide facts and data on the impact of the proposed local sales tax on consumer purchases; and</p>
          <p>(5) provide facts and data related to the individual programs and projects to be funded with the local sales tax.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Local sales taxes are to be used instead of traditional local revenues only for construction and rehabilitation of capital projects when a clear regional benefit beyond the taxing jurisdiction can be demonstrated. Use of local sales tax revenues for local projects decreases the benefits to taxpayers of the deductibility of local property taxes and the state assistance provided through the property tax refund system and increases the fiscal inequities between similar communities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Local resolution before application for authority.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before the governing body of a political subdivision requests legislative approval to impose a local sales tax authorized by a special law, it shall adopt a resolution indicating its approval of the tax. The resolution must include the following information:</p>
          <p>(1) the proposed tax rate;</p>
          <p>(2) a detailed description of no more than five capital projects that will be funded with revenue from the tax;</p>
          <p>(3) documentation of the regional significance of each project, including the share of the economic benefit to or use of each project by persons residing, or businesses located, outside of the jurisdiction;</p>
          <p>(4) the amount of local sales tax revenue that would be used for each project and the estimated time needed to raise that amount of revenue; and</p>
          <p>(5) the total revenue that will be raised for all projects before the tax expires, and the estimated length of time that the tax will be in effect if all proposed projects are funded.</p>
          <p>(b) The jurisdiction seeking authority to impose a local sales tax by special law must submit the resolution in paragraph (a) along with underlying documentation indicating how the benefits under paragraph (a), clause (3), were determined, to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over taxes no later than January 31 of the year in which the jurisdiction is seeking a special law authorizing the tax.</p>
          <p>(c) The special legislation granting local sales tax authority is not required to allow funding for all projects listed in the resolution with the revenue from the local sales tax, but must not include any projects not contained in the resolution.</p>
          <p>(d) For purposes of this section, a "capital project" or "project" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a single building or structure including associated infrastructure needed to safely access or use the building or structure;</p>
          <p>(2) improvements within a single park or named recreation area; or</p>
          <p>(3) a contiguous trail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Legislative authority required before voter approval; requirements for adoption, use, termination.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A political subdivision must receive legislative authority to impose a local sales tax before submitting the tax for approval by voters of the political subdivision. Imposition of a local sales tax is subject to approval by voters of the political subdivision at a general election. The election must be conducted at a general election within the two-year period after the governing body of the political subdivision has received authority to impose the tax. If the authorizing legislation allows the tax to be imposed for more than one project, there must be a separate question approving the use of the tax revenue for each project. Notwithstanding the authorizing legislation, a project that is not approved by the voters may not be funded with the local sales tax revenue and the termination date of the tax set in the authorizing legislation must be reduced proportionately based on the share of that project's cost to the total costs of all projects included in the authorizing legislation.</p>
          <p>(b) The proceeds of the tax must be dedicated exclusively to payment of the construction and rehabilitation costs and associated bonding costs related to the specific capital improvement projects that were approved by the voters under paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(c) The tax must terminate after the revenues raised are sufficient to fund the projects approved by the voters under paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(d) After a sales tax imposed by a political subdivision has expired or been terminated, the political subdivision is prohibited from imposing a local sales tax for a period of one year.</p>
          <p>(e) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), if a political subdivision received voter approval to seek authority for a local sales tax at the November 6, 2018, general election and is granted authority to impose a local sales tax before January 1, 2021, the tax may be imposed without an additional referendum provided that it meets the requirements of subdivision 2 and the list of specific projects contained in the resolution does not conflict with the projects listed in the approving referendum.</p>
          <p>(f) If a tax is terminated because sufficient revenues have been raised, any amount of tax collected under subdivision 9, after sufficient revenues have been raised and before the quarterly termination required under subdivision 12, paragraph (a), that is greater than the average quarterly revenues collected over the immediately preceding 12 calendar months must be retained by the commissioner for deposit in the general fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Temporary moratorium.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding subdivisions 1, 2, and 3, until after May 31, 2025, a political subdivision may not engage in any of the following activities in connection with imposing a new local sales and use tax or modifying an existing local sales and use tax:</p>
          <p>(1) any activity described in subdivision 1, paragraph (d);</p>
          <p>(2) adopt a resolution; or</p>
          <p>(3) seek voter approval.</p>
          <p>(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply to new local sales and use taxes or modifications to existing local sales and use taxes authorized in May, 2023.</p>
          <p>(c) This subdivision expires June 1, 2025.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Tax base.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The tax applies to sales taxable under this chapter that occur within the political subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) Taxable goods or services are subject to a political subdivision's sales tax, if they are sourced to the political subdivision pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.668">297A.668</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Tax rate.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The tax rate is as specified in the special law authorization and as imposed by the political subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) The full political subdivision rate applies to any sales that are taxed at a state rate, and the political subdivision must not have more than one local sales tax rate or more than one local use tax rate. This paragraph does not apply to sales or use taxes imposed on electricity, piped natural or artificial gas, or other heating fuels delivered by the seller, or the retail sale or transfer of motor vehicles, aircraft, watercraft, modular homes, manufactured homes, or mobile homes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Use tax.</span></h2>
          <p>A compensating use tax applies, at the same rate as the sales tax, on the use, storage, distribution, or consumption of tangible personal property or taxable services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) All goods or services that are otherwise exempt from taxation under this chapter are exempt from a political subdivision's tax.</p>
          <p>(b) All mobile transportation equipment, and parts and accessories attached to or to be attached to the equipment are exempt, if purchased by a holder of a motor carrier direct pay permit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.90">297A.90</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Credit for other local taxes.</span></h2>
          <p>If a person paid sales or use tax to another political subdivision of this state on an item subject to tax under this section, a credit applies against the tax imposed under this section. The credit equals the tax the person paid to the other political subdivision for the item.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Enforcement; collection; and administration.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of revenue shall collect the taxes subject to this section. The commissioner may collect the tax with the state sales and use tax. All taxes under this section are subject to the same penalties, interest, and enforcement provisions as apply to the state sales and use tax.</p>
          <p>(b) A request for a refund of state sales tax paid in excess of the amount of tax legally due includes a request for a refund of the political subdivision taxes paid on the goods or services. The commissioner shall refund to the taxpayer the full amount of the political subdivision taxes paid on exempt sales or use.</p>
          <p>(c) A political subdivision shall incur a legal debt to the state for refunds of local sales taxes made by the commissioner after a tax has terminated when the amount of the refunds exceeds the amount of local sales taxes collected for but not remitted to the political subdivision. The commissioner of revenue shall bill the political subdivision for this difference. The commissioner shall deposit the money in the state treasury and credit it to the general fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Use of zip code in determining location of sale.</span></h2>
          <p>The lowest combined tax rate imposed in the zip code area applies if the area includes more than one tax rate in any level of taxing jurisdictions. If a nine-digit zip code designation is not available for a street address or if a seller is unable to determine the nine-digit zip code designation of a purchaser after exercising due diligence to determine the designation, the seller may apply the rate for the five-digit zip code area. For the purposes of this subdivision, there is a rebuttable presumption that a seller has exercised due diligence if the seller has attempted to determine the nine-digit zip code designation by utilizing software approved by the governing board that makes this designation from the street address and the five-digit zip code of the purchaser. Notwithstanding subdivision 13, this subdivision applies to all local sales taxes without regard to the date of authorization. This subdivision does not apply when the purchased product is received by the purchaser at the business location of the seller.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Revenues; cost of collection.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall remit the proceeds of the tax, less refunds and a proportionate share of the cost of collection, at least quarterly, to the political subdivision. The commissioner shall deduct from the proceeds remitted an amount that equals</p>
          <p>(1) the direct and indirect costs of the department to administer, audit, and collect the political subdivision's tax, plus</p>
          <p>(2) the political subdivision's proportionate share of the indirect cost of administering all taxes under this section, plus</p>
          <p>(3) the cost of constructing and maintaining a zip code or geo-code database necessary for local sales tax collections under the Streamlined Sales and Use Tax Agreement in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.995">297A.995</a>.</p>
          <p>The initial cost of constructing a database under clause (3) shall be distributed among the cities with a local sales tax based on each city's population. The commissioner shall develop a method for distributing the cost of maintaining the database among the cities with a local sales tax based on the number of boundary changes for each city.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Effective dates; notification.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A political subdivision may impose a tax under this section starting only on the first day of a calendar quarter. A political subdivision may repeal a tax under this section stopping only on the last day of a calendar quarter.</p>
          <p>(b) The political subdivision shall notify the commissioner of revenue at least 90 days before imposing, changing the rate of, or repealing a tax under this section.</p>
          <p>(c) The political subdivision shall change the rate of tax imposed under this section starting only on the first day of a calendar quarter, and only after the commissioner has notified sellers at least 60 days prior to the change.</p>
          <p>(d) The political subdivision shall apply the rate change for sales tax imposed under this section to purchases from printed catalogs, wherein the purchaser computed the tax based upon local tax rates published in the catalog, starting only on the first day of a calendar quarter, and only after the commissioner has notified sellers at least 120 days prior to the change.</p>
          <p>(e) The political subdivision shall apply local jurisdiction boundary changes to taxes imposed under this section starting only on the first day of a calendar quarter, and only after the commissioner has notified sellers at least 60 days prior to the change.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.12a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12a.<span class="headnote">Notification of use tax.</span></h2>
          <p>Any political subdivision imposing a local sales and use tax, which maintains an official website, must display on its main home page a link to a notice that residents and businesses in the political subdivision may owe a local use tax on purchases of goods and services made outside of the political subdivision limits. The notice must provide information, including a link to any relevant Department of Revenue website, on how the taxpayer may get information and forms necessary for calculating and paying the tax. If the political subdivision provides and bills for sewer, water, garbage collection, or other public utility services, the billing statement must also include at least once per year a notice that residents and businesses may owe a local use tax on purchases made outside of the political subdivision limits and provide information on how the taxpayer may get information and forms necessary for calculating and paying the tax.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.99.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>This section applies to all local sales taxes that were authorized before, on, or after June 2, 1997.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.99.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.42.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.81.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 81</a>-83; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.1.28.0">2003 c 127 art 1 s 28</a>-30; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.7.22.0">2005 c 151 art 7 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/3/#laws.5.22.0">1Sp2005 c 3 art 5 s 22</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/259/#laws.6.30.0">2006 c 259 art 6 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/152/#laws.4.1.0">2008 c 152 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.7.7.0">2008 c 366 art 7 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/7/#laws.4.1.0">1Sp2011 c 7 art 4 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.8.43.0">2013 c 143 art 8 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.6.1.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 6 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.8.1.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/37/#laws.5.1.0">2023 c 37 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.10.1.0">2023 c 64 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.3.28.0">2023 c 68 art 3 s 28</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.9905">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.9905 USE OF LOCAL TAX REVENUES BY CITIES OF THE FIRST CLASS.</h1>
        <p>(a) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.99">297A.99</a>, or other general or special law or charter provision, if the revenues from any local tax imposed on retail sales under special law by a city of the first class exceeds the amount needed to fund the uses authorized in the special law, the city may expend the excess revenue from the tax to fund other capital projects of regional significance.</p>
        <p>(b) For purposes of this section:</p>
        <p>(1) "city of the first class" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/410.01">410.01</a>; and</p>
        <p>(2) "capital project of regional significance" means construction, expansion, or renovation of a sports facility or convention or civic center that meets the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.71#stat.297A.71.44">297A.71, subdivision 44</a>, paragraph (a).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.9905.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/299/#laws.5.6.0">2012 c 299 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.3.38.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 3 s 38</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.991">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.991 REPORTING OF SALES TAX ON MINNESOTA GOVERNMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.991.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner of revenue to report.</span></h2>
          <p>For each fiscal year, the commissioner shall estimate the amount of revenues derived from imposing the tax under this chapter and chapter 297B on state agencies and political subdivisions. The commissioner shall report this amount to the commissioner of management and budget before the time for filing reports for the fiscal year with the United States Department of Commerce.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.991.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner of management and budget to report.</span></h2>
          <p>In reporting the sales tax and sales tax on motor vehicles collections to the United States Department of Commerce, the commissioner of management and budget shall exclude the estimated amount from the sales and motor vehicle collections. Sales tax and sales tax on motor vehicles revenues received from political subdivisions must be reported as intergovernmental grants or similar intergovernmental revenue. The amount of the sales tax and sales tax on motor vehicles paid by state agencies must be reported as reduced state expenditures.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.991.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.43.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.9915">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.9915 REGIONAL TRANSPORTATION SALES AND USE TAX.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.9915.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Metropolitan area" means the counties of Anoka, Carver, Dakota, Hennepin, Ramsey, Scott, and Washington.</p>
          <p>(c) "Metropolitan Council" or "council" means the Metropolitan Council established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.123">473.123</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Regional transportation sales tax" means the regional transportation sales and use tax imposed under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.9915.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Sales tax imposition; rate.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.123#stat.473.123.1">473.123, subdivision 1</a>, the Metropolitan Council must impose a regional transportation sales and use tax at a rate of three-quarters of one percent on retail sales and uses taxable under this chapter made in the metropolitan area or to a destination in the metropolitan area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.9915.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Administration; collection; enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided in this section, the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.99">297A.99, subdivisions 4</a>, and 6 to 12a, govern the administration, collection, and enforcement of the regional transportation sales tax.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.9915.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Deposit.</span></h2>
          <p>Proceeds of the regional transportation sales tax must be allocated as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) 83 percent to the Metropolitan Council for the purposes specified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.4465">473.4465</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) 17 percent to metropolitan counties, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.49#stat.174.49.1">174.49, subdivision 1</a>, in the manner provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.49#stat.174.49.5">174.49, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.9915.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Revenue bonds.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In addition to other authority granted in this section, and notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.39#stat.473.39.7">473.39, subdivision 7</a>, or any other law to the contrary, the council may, by resolution, authorize the sale and issuance of revenue bonds, notes, or obligations to provide funds to (1) implement the council's transit capital improvement program, and (2) refund bonds issued under this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) The bonds are payable from and secured by a pledge of all or part of the revenue received under subdivision 4, clause (1), and associated investment earnings on debt proceeds. The council may, by resolution, authorize the issuance of the bonds as general obligations of the council. The bonds must be sold, issued, and secured in the manner provided in chapter 475, and the council has the same powers and duties as a municipality and its governing body in issuing bonds under chapter 475, except that no election is required and the net debt limitations in chapter 475 do not apply to such bonds. The proceeds of the bonds may also be used to fund necessary reserves and to pay credit enhancement fees, issuance costs, and other financing costs during the life of the debt.</p>
          <p>(c) The bonds may be secured by a bond resolution, or a trust indenture entered into by the council with a corporate trustee within or outside the state, which must define the revenues and bond proceeds pledged for the payment and security of the bonds. The pledge must be a valid charge on the revenues received under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.99#stat.297A.99.11">297A.99, subdivision 11</a>. Neither the state, nor any municipality or political subdivision except the council, nor any member or officer or employee of the council, is liable on the obligations. No mortgage or security interest in any tangible real or personal property is granted to the bondholders or the trustee, but they have a valid security interest in the revenues and bond proceeds received by the council and pledged to the payment of the bonds. In the bond resolution or trust indenture, the council may make such covenants as it determines to be reasonable for the protection of the bondholders.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.9915.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.3.29.0">2023 c 68 art 3 s 29</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.992">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.992 METROPOLITAN TRANSPORTATION AREA SALES TAX.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) "metropolitan transportation area" means the counties participating in the joint powers agreement under subdivision 3;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) "eligible county" means the county of Anoka, Carver, Dakota, Hennepin, Ramsey, Scott, or Washington;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) "committee" means the Grant Evaluation and Ranking System (GEARS) Committee;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) "minimum guarantee county" means any metropolitan county or eligible county that is participating in the joint powers agreement under subdivision 3, whose proportion of the annual sales tax revenue under this section collected within that county is less than or equal to three percent; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) "population" means the population, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/477A.011#stat.477A.011.3">477A.011, subdivision 3</a>, estimated or established by July 15 of the year prior to the calendar year in which the representatives will serve on the Grant Evaluation and Ranking System Committee established under subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Authorization; rates.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.99">297A.99</a>, subdivisions 1, 2, and 3, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/477A.016">477A.016</a>, or any other law, the board of a county participating in a joint powers agreement as specified in this section shall impose by resolution (1) a transportation sales and use tax at a rate of one-quarter of one percent on retail sales and uses taxable under this chapter, and (2) an excise tax of $20 per motor vehicle, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297B.01#stat.297B.01.11">297B.01, subdivision 11</a>, purchased or acquired from any person engaged in the business of selling motor vehicles at retail, occurring within the jurisdiction of the taxing authority. The taxes authorized are to fund transportation improvements as specified in this section, including debt service on obligations issued to finance such improvements pursuant to subdivision 7.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The tax imposed under this section is not included in determining if the total tax on lodging in the city of Minneapolis exceeds the maximum allowed tax under Laws 1986, chapter 396, section 5, as amended by Laws 2001, First Special Session chapter 5, article 12, section 87, or in determining a tax that may be imposed under any other limitations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Joint powers agreement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Before imposing the taxes authorized in subdivision 2, an eligible county must declare by resolution of its county board to be part of the metropolitan transportation area and must enter into a joint powers agreement. The joint powers agreement:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) must form a joint powers board, as specified in subdivision 4;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) must provide a process that allows any eligible county, by resolution of its county board, to join the joint powers board and impose the taxes authorized in subdivision 2;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) may provide for withdrawal of a participating county before final termination of the agreement; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) may provide for a weighted voting system for joint powers board decisions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Joint powers board.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The joint powers board must consist of one or more commissioners of each county that is in the metropolitan transportation area, appointed by its county board, and the chair of the Metropolitan Council, who must have voting rights, subject to subdivision 3, clause (4). The joint powers board has the powers and duties provided in this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The joint powers board may utilize no more than three-fourths of one percent of the proceeds of the taxes imposed under this section for ordinary administrative expenses incurred in carrying out the provisions of this section. Any additional administrative expenses must be paid by the participating counties.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The joint powers board may establish a technical advisory group that is separate from the GEARS Committee. The group must consist of representatives of cities, counties, or public agencies, including the Metropolitan Council. The technical advisory group must be used solely for technical consultation purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Grant application and awards; Grant Evaluation and Ranking System (GEARS) Committee.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The joint powers board shall establish a grant application process and identify the amount of available funding for grant awards. Grant applications must be submitted in a form prescribed by the joint powers board. An applicant must provide, in addition to all other information required by the joint powers board, the estimated cost of the project, the amount of the grant sought, possible sources of funding in addition to the grant sought, and identification of any federal funds that will be utilized if the grant is awarded. A grant application seeking transit capital funding must identify the source of money necessary to operate the transit improvement.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The joint powers board shall establish a timeline and procedures for the award of grants, and may award grants only to the state and political subdivisions. The board shall define objective criteria for the award of grants, which must include, but not be limited to, consistency with the most recent version of the transportation policy plan adopted by the Metropolitan Council under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.146">473.146</a>. The joint powers board shall maximize the availability and use of federal funds in projects funded under this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The joint powers board shall establish a GEARS Committee, which must consist of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) one county commissioner from each county that is in the metropolitan transportation area, appointed by its county board;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) one elected city representative from each county that is in the metropolitan transportation area;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) one additional elected city representative from each county for every additional 400,000 in population, or fraction of 400,000, in the county that is above 400,000 in population; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the chair of the Metropolitan Council Transportation Committee.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Each city representative must be elected at a meeting of cities in the metropolitan transportation area, which must be convened for that purpose by the Association of Metropolitan Municipalities.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The committee shall evaluate grant applications following objective criteria established by the joint powers board, and must provide to the joint powers board a selection list of transportation projects that includes a priority ranking.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) A grant award for a transit project located within the metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>, may be funded only after the Metropolitan Council reviews the project for consistency with the transit portion of the Metropolitan Council policy plan and one of the following occurs:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the Metropolitan Council finds the project to be consistent;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the Metropolitan Council initially finds the project to be inconsistent, but after a good faith effort to resolve the inconsistency through negotiations with the joint powers board, agrees that the grant award may be funded; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the Metropolitan Council finds the project to be inconsistent, and submits the consistency issue for final determination to a panel, which determines the project to be consistent. The panel is composed of a member appointed by the chair of the Metropolitan Council, a member appointed by the joint powers board, and a member agreed upon by both the chair and the joint powers board.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) Grants must be funded by the proceeds of the taxes imposed under this section, bonds, notes, or other obligations issued by the joint powers board under subdivision 7.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) Notwithstanding the provisions of this section except subdivision 6a, of the revenue collected under this section, the joint powers board shall allocate to the Metropolitan Council, in fiscal years 2012 and 2013, an amount not less than 75 percent of the net cost of operations for those transitways that were receiving metropolitan sales tax funds through an operating grant agreement on June 30, 2011.</p>
          <p>(i) The Metropolitan Council shall expend any funds allocated under paragraph (h) for the operations of the specified transitways solely within those counties that are in the metropolitan transportation area.</p>
          <p class="in">(j) Nothing in paragraph (h) or (i) prevents grant awards to the Metropolitan Council for capital and operating assistance for transitways and park-and-ride facilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Allocation of grant awards.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The board must allocate grant awards only for the following transit purposes:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) capital improvements to transitways, including, but not limited to, commuter rail rolling stock, light rail vehicles, and transitway buses;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) capital costs for park-and-ride facilities, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.256">174.256</a>, subdivision 2;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) feasibility studies, planning, alternatives analyses, environmental studies, engineering, property acquisition for transitway purposes, and construction of transitways; and</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) operating assistance for transitways.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The joint powers board must annually award grants to each minimum guarantee county in an amount no less than the amount of sales tax revenue collected within that county.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) No more than 1.25 percent of the total awards may be annually allocated for planning, studies, design, construction, maintenance, and operation of pedestrian programs and bicycle programs and pathways.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Priority of fund uses.</span></h2>
          <p>The joint powers board shall allocate all revenues from the taxes imposed under this section in conformance with the following priority order:</p>
          <p>(1) payment of debt service necessary for the fiscal year on bonds or other obligations issued prior to January 1, 2011, under subdivision 7; and</p>
          <p>(2) as otherwise authorized under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Bonds.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The joint powers board or any county, acting under a joint powers agreement as specified in this section, may, by resolution, authorize, issue, and sell its bonds, notes, or other obligations for the purpose of funding grants under subdivision 6. The joint powers board or county may also, by resolution, issue bonds to refund the bonds issued pursuant to this subdivision.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The bonds of the joint powers board must be limited obligations, payable solely from or secured by taxes levied under this section.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The bonds of any county may be limited obligations, payable solely from or secured by taxes levied under this section. A county may also pledge its full faith, credit, and taxing power as additional security for the bonds.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) Bonds may be issued in one or more series and sold without an election. The bonds shall be secured, bear the interest rate or rates or a variable rate, have the rank or priority, be executed in the manner, be payable in the manner, mature, and be subject to the defaults, redemptions, repurchases, tender options, or other terms, and shall be sold in such manner as the joint powers board, the regional railroad authority, or the county may determine.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The joint powers board or any regional railroad authority or any county may enter into and perform all contracts deemed necessary or desirable by it to issue and secure the bonds, including an indenture of trust with a trustee within or without the state.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) Except as otherwise provided in this subdivision, the bonds must be issued and sold in the manner provided under chapter 475.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) The joint powers board or any regional railroad authority wholly within the metropolitan transportation area also may authorize, issue, and sell its bonds, notes, or other obligations for the purposes, and in accordance with the procedures, set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/398A.07">398A.07</a> to fund grants as provided in subdivision 6. The bonds of any regional railroad authority may be limited obligations, payable solely from or secured by taxes levied under this section. A regional railroad authority may also pledge its taxing powers as additional security for the bonds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Allocation of revenues.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">After the deductions allowed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.99">297A.99</a>, subdivision 11, the commissioner of revenue shall remit the proceeds of the taxes imposed under this section on a monthly basis, as directed by the joint powers board under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Administration, collection, enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Except as otherwise provided in this section, the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.99">297A.99, subdivisions 4</a> and 6 to 12a, govern the administration, collection, and enforcement of the tax authorized under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Termination of taxes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The taxes imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.99">297A.99</a>, subdivision 1, by a county that withdraws from the joint powers agreement pursuant to subdivision 3, clause (3), shall terminate when the county has satisfied its portion, as defined in the joint powers agreement, of all outstanding bonds or obligations entered into while the county was a member of the agreement.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If the joint powers agreement under subdivision 3 is terminated, the taxes imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.99#stat.297A.99.1">297A.99, subdivision 1</a>, at the time of the agreement termination will terminate when all outstanding bonds or obligations are satisfied. The auditors of the counties in which the taxes are imposed shall see to the administration of this paragraph.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.10a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10a.<span class="headnote">Termination of taxes; use of remaining funds.</span></h2>
          <p>If the joint powers agreement under subdivision 3 is terminated, funds received by a county in association with the termination may be used for any of the purposes specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.993">297A.993, subdivision </a>2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The joint powers board shall report annually by February 1 to the house of representatives and senate committees having jurisdiction over transportation policy and finance concerning the revenues received and grants awarded.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.992.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Grant awards to Metropolitan Council.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Any grant award under this section made to the Metropolitan Council must supplement, and must not supplant, operating and capital assistance provided by the state.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.992.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/152/#laws.4.2.0">2008 c 152 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.8.3.0">2009 c 88 art 8 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/3/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp2011 c 3 art 2 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/3/#laws.3.111.0">1Sp2017 c 3 art 3 s 111</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.9925">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.9925 METROPOLITAN REGION SALES AND USE TAX.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.9925.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Metropolitan Council" or "council" means the Metropolitan Council established by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.123">473.123</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) "Metropolitan county" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.4">473.121, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Metropolitan sales tax" means the metropolitan region sales and use tax imposed under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.9925.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Sales tax imposition; rate.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.123#stat.473.123.1">473.123, subdivision 1</a>, the Metropolitan Council must impose a metropolitan region sales and use tax at a rate of 0.25 percent on retail sales made in the metropolitan counties or to a destination in the metropolitan counties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.9925.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Administration; collection; enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided in this section, the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.99">297A.99, subdivisions 4</a>, and 6 to 12a, govern the administration, collection, and enforcement of the metropolitan sales tax.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.9925.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Distribution.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.94">297A.94</a>, proceeds of the metropolitan sales tax are distributed:</p>
          <p>(1) 25 percent to the state rent assistance account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462A.2095">462A.2095</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) 25 percent to the metropolitan city aid account in the housing assistance fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/477A.37">477A.37</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) 50 percent to the metropolitan county aid account in the housing assistance fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/477A.37">477A.37</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.297A.9925.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/37/#laws.5.2.0">2023 c 37 art 5 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.297A.993">
        <h1 class="shn">297A.993 COUNTY TRANSPORTATION SALES AND USE TAX.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.993.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization; rates.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.99">297A.99</a>, subdivisions 1, 2, 3, 5, and 13, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/477A.016">477A.016</a>, or any other law, the board of a county, or more than one county acting under a joint powers agreement, may by resolution of the county board, or each of the county boards, following a public hearing impose (1) a transportation sales tax at a rate of up to one-half of one percent on retail sales and uses taxable under this chapter, and (2) an excise tax of $20 per motor vehicle, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297B.01#stat.297B.01.11">297B.01, subdivision 11</a>, purchased or acquired from any person engaged in the business of selling motor vehicles at retail, occurring within the jurisdiction of the taxing authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.297A.993.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Allocation; termination.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The proceeds of the taxes must be dedicated exclusively to: (1) payment of the capital cost of a specific transportation project or improvement; (2) payment of the costs, which may include both capital and operating costs, of a specific transit project or improvement; (3) payment of the capital costs of a safe routes to school program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.40">174.40</a>; (4) payment of transit operating costs; or (5) payment of the capital cost of constructing buildings and other facilities for maintaining transportation or transit projects or improvements. The transportation or transit project or improvement must be designated by the board of the county, or more than one county acting under a joint powers agreement.
